## 1. What this report holds and how to read it

A collection: the member words, the Strong's codes behind them, and what the study has recorded for the occurrences of those words, from the base data through the observations and the claims into the narrative. Sections 2 and 3 say what the data is; 4 and 5 say what the study has recorded; 6 lists the phrases; 7 lists the faults found in the stored rows. Verses are in Scripture order (book in cfg_book_order, chapter, verse). The detail that does not fit a reading (every Strong's entry in full, every observation and node, the change history, the narrative text) is in the workbook, one sheet for each listing; the sheet is named where it is cited.

## 3. Each Strong's code behind the collection

Each section is one code: what it means as stored, the member words that carry it, and its occurrences in Scripture order with the verse and the member word marked. The occurrences where the member surface is only shared with another code's unit are in section 3.144.

### 3.1 H3372G יָרֵא ya.re "to fear": 182 own occurrences in 178 verses

- **Meaning as stored:** Hebrew; native word יָרֵא, transliteration ya.re; STEP gloss "to fear"; sense head "frightening(DANGER)".
- **Senses (first level of the meaning tree of the lemma H3372):** to fear, revere, be afraid; (TWOT) to shoot, pour.
- **Related terms (7):** H3372H ya.re "to fear: revere"; H3373 ya.re "afraid"; H3374 yir.ah "fear"; H3375 yir.on "Yiron"; H4172A mo.ra "fear"; H4172B mo.rah "fear"; more in the workbook.
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M01 Fear & Awe (None).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 208; verses STEP attests 200; verse_lexical rows the parse found 204.

- **Carried by the member words:** fear (88), afraid (75), feared (15), frighten (2), fearful (1), fears (1).
- **Engagement of these occurrences:** none recorded 182.

### 3.2 G5399 φοβέω fobeō "to fear": 90 own occurrences in 87 verses

- **Meaning as stored:** Greek; native word φοβέω, transliteration fobeō; STEP gloss "to fear"; sense head "to fear" (the code is its own lemma).
- **Related terms (9):** G0870 afobōs "fearlessly"; G1629 ekfobeō "to terrify"; G1719 emfobos "afraid"; G4422 ptoeō "to frighten"; G4423 ptoēsis "fear"; G4426 pturomai "to frighten"; more in the workbook.
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M01 Fear & Awe (None).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 441; verses STEP attests 91; verse_lexical rows the parse found 94.

- **Carried by the member words:** fear (37), afraid (34), feared (10), fearing (5), be afraid (1), fears (1), frightened (1), whoever fears (1).
- **Engagement of these occurrences:** none recorded 90.

### 3.3 H3372H יָרֵא ya.re "to fear: revere": 70 own occurrences in 69 verses

- **Meaning as stored:** Hebrew; native word יָרֵא, transliteration ya.re; STEP gloss "to fear: revere"; sense head "awesome(god)".
- **Senses (first level of the meaning tree of the lemma H3372):** to fear, revere, be afraid; (TWOT) to shoot, pour.
- **Related terms (7):** H3372G ya.re "to fear"; H3373 ya.re "afraid"; H3374 yir.ah "fear"; H3375 yir.on "Yiron"; H4172A mo.ra "fear"; H4172B mo.rah "fear"; more in the workbook.
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M01 Fear & Awe (None).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 111; verses STEP attests 107; verse_lexical rows the parse found 110.

- **Carried by the member words:** fear (55), feared (11), afraid (1), fearfully (1), fearing (1), fears (1).
- **Engagement of these occurrences:** none recorded 70.

## 4. What the study has recorded for these occurrences

Of 1238 occurrences, 7 have an engagement observation and 1231 have none recorded. There are 880 live observations on them (1 engagement, 843 of other recorded stages, 36 of the retired catalogue stages) and 32 withdrawn.

### 4.1 Engagement: what each word does for the inner being (1 observations)

An engagement observation states, for the surface in a verse, whether it voices an inner-being operation (use, face) or is silent. Each is one statement applied to every occurrence it covers.

- **Observation 21584** (engagement, tag statement, status draft): Fret names the inner operation of heating oneself with anger or envy over the prospering wicked: do not fret.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 37:1 (fret), Psa 37:7 (fret), Psa 37:8 (fret), Pro 24:19 (fret)

### 4.2 Other recorded observations (843)

Observations of the stages that carry a written finding (prior-capture, reading, strand-reading and the like), each with the occurrences it applies to.

- **Observation 20836** (Analysis_phase_1, tag face, status draft): Fear here is being afraid of harm from people (tree H3372 1a1 'to fear, be afraid').
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20840** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): The fear holds him back from naming her as his wife: it is a refusal to say it.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20842** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): What he feared is that the men would kill him because of Rebekah.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20844** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): The fear shows in what he said: that she is his sister.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20846** (Analysis_phase_1, tag party, status draft): Isaac fears the men of the place, and fears for his life at their hands.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20848** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): Isaac has settled in Gerar (Gen 26:6) and is among strangers when the men ask.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20850** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): Isaac feared to call her his wife and thought the men might kill him because of her.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20852** (Analysis_phase_1, tag link, status draft): The men asked; he answered she is his sister; his reason is the fear, and the fear is that they would kill him because of Rebekah, who was attractive.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20853** (Analysis_phase_1, tag link, status draft): The question about his wife is what he fears to answer truly.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 20854** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): Discovery: whether the men meant any harm is not stated; the passage is needed.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 26:7 (feared)
- **Observation 21057** (Analysis_phase_1, tag face, status draft): Fear here is reverent awe before the Lord (tree H3372 1a3 'to fear, reverence, honour, respect').
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21063** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): The fear is required, a pursuit set before Israel.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21068** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): The one feared is the Lord their God.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21074** (Analysis_phase_1, tag party, status draft): Israel is to fear the Lord, who is the one feared.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21077** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): The speaker turns to this after recalling forty days on the mountain and the Lord's unwillingness to destroy them (Deu 10:10-11).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21079** (Analysis_phase_1, tag statement, status draft): Israel is to fear the Lord.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21083** (Analysis_phase_1, tag link, status draft): The one question is answered by four infinitives: to fear, to walk, to love and to serve.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 10:12 (fear)
- **Observation 21084** (Analysis_phase_1, tag link, status draft): The four are one requirement, not four separate ones.

  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 12:3 (tremble)
- **Observation 12554** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Dread and trembling arise before God himself: before his presence, his voice in the thunder, his word and his acts. Job is terrified at his presence and could not face his majesty; the camp at Sinai trembles; Daniel's companions flee and Daniel is left trembling; Joseph's brothers turn to one another in fear at what God has done; sinners in Zion ask who can live beside the consuming fire.
  - applies to 12 occurrence(s): Gen 42:28 (trembling), Exo 19:16 (trembled), Job 13:11 (dread), Job 23:15 (terrified), Job 23:15 (dread), Job 23:16 (terrified), Job 31:23 (terror), Job 37:1 (trembles), Isa 33:14 (afraid), Isa 33:14 (trembling), Dan 10:7 (trembling), Dan 10:11 (trembling)
- **Observation 12555** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Trembling comes at an arrival or a discovery: elders and a priest come trembling to meet Samuel and David, Adonijah's guests rise and scatter, Isaac trembles on learning who brought the game, a man in the night is startled, a people follows in trembling, and towns flee before an advancing army.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Rut 3:8 (startled), 1Sa 13:7 (trembling), 1Sa 16:4 (trembling), 1Sa 21:1 (trembling), 1Ki 1:49 (trembled), Isa 10:29 (trembles)
- **Observation 12556** (prior-capture, tag party, status draft): God lays dread on whole peoples, and the verses give what follows: no one can stand against Israel, the kingdoms make no war against Jehoshaphat, the peoples are still as a stone, Egypt is glad when Israel leaves, kings who see Zion are seized with trembling, and the prayer is that the nations tremble at his presence.
  - applies to 15 occurrence(s): Exo 15:14 (tremble), Exo 15:15 (trembling), Exo 15:16 (terror), Exo 15:16 (dread), Deu 11:25 (fear), Deu 11:25 (dread), 1Ch 14:17 (fear), 2Ch 17:10 (fear), 2Ch 20:29 (fear), Psa 48:6 (trembling), Psa 105:38 (dread), Isa 19:16 (fear); and 3 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 12557** (prior-capture, tag party, status draft): Fear is also done to others as an act, with the one who frightens as the subject: Ahithophel's plan to throw the king's company into a panic, Gideon throwing an army into panic, sorceries from which Babylon hopes to inspire terror, God's justice so that man may strike terror no more, creditors roused to make the debtor tremble, Job asking whether God will frighten a driven leaf, those sent to terrify the nations.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Judg 8:12 (panic), 2Sa 17:2 (panic), Job 13:25 (frighten), Psa 10:18 (strike terror), Isa 47:12 (inspire terror), Eze 30:9 (terrify), Hab 2:7 (tremble), Zec 1:21 (terrify)
- **Observation 12558** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 'Do not be in dread' is spoken with its ground beside it: the Lord is in their midst, goes with them, or has said it from of old. The same word is turned to oneself as trust: I will not be afraid, whom shall I fear.
  - applies to 13 occurrence(s): Deu 1:29 (dread), Deu 1:29 (afraid), Deu 7:21 (dread), Deu 20:3 (fear), Deu 20:3 (dread), Deu 31:6 (fear), Deu 31:6 (dread), Jos 1:9 (frightened), Psa 27:1 (fear), Psa 27:1 (afraid), Isa 12:2 (afraid), Isa 44:8 (fear); and 1 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 12559** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 'None shall make you afraid' is the promise of peace: lying down in the land, sitting under the vine and fig tree, dwelling securely, returning in quiet, being led in safety; Zophar offers it to Job as the outcome of turning.
  - applies to 9 occurrence(s): Lev 26:6 (afraid), Job 11:19 (afraid), Psa 78:53 (afraid), Jer 30:10 (afraid), Jer 46:27 (afraid), Eze 34:28 (make them afraid), Eze 39:26 (make them afraid), Mic 4:4 (make them afraid), Zep 3:13 (make them afraid)
- **Observation 12560** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): The dread root appears in joy: the heart thrills and exults, and the people are struck with awe and trembling at the good and the prosperity provided for them once their guilt is forgiven, beside rejoicing with trembling.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 2:11 (fear), Psa 2:11 (trembling), Jer 33:9 (fear), Jer 33:9 (tremble)
- **Observation 12561** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Trembling is called for and decreed: the earth is told to tremble before the Lord in his holiness, the peoples at his reign, the inhabitants of the land at the coming day; the women at ease are told to shudder; Darius decrees that all tremble before the God of Daniel; the heavens are told to be appalled.
  - applies to 11 occurrence(s): 1Ch 16:30 (tremble), Psa 96:9 (tremble), Psa 99:1 (tremble), Psa 114:7 (tremble), Isa 32:10 (shudder), Isa 32:11 (tremble), Isa 32:11 (shudder), Jer 2:12 (appalled), Dan 6:26 (to tremble), Dan 6:26 (fear), Joe 2:1 (tremble)
- **Observation 12562** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The physical world and the dead tremble before God, with the words used of people: mountains quake at his anger, the deep trembles when the waters see him, the pillars of heaven tremble at his rebuke, the dead tremble under the waters, and the land trembles at those who trample the needy.
  - applies to 23 occurrence(s): Exo 19:18 (trembled), 1Sa 14:15 (panic), 1Sa 14:15 (trembled), 1Sa 14:15 (quaked), 2Sa 22:8 (trembled), Job 9:6 (shakes), Job 9:6 (tremble), Job 26:5 (tremble), Job 26:11 (tremble), Psa 18:7 (trembled), Psa 29:8 (shakes), Psa 77:16 (afraid); and 11 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 12563** (prior-capture, tag term, status draft): One Hebrew root, ragaz, 'to tremble', is rendered in the ESV as trembling, anger, raging, quarrelling, grief and disturbance: peoples and earth tremble, the psalmist is told to be angry and not sin, Sennacherib is said to have raged against God, Joseph tells his brothers not to quarrel, David is deeply moved at Absalom's death, Samuel is disturbed at being brought up, Sheol is stirred up.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Exo 15:14 (tremble), Psa 18:7 (trembled), Hab 3:16 (trembles), Hab 3:16 (tremble)
- **Observation 12565** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): What it is turned toward: dread is forbidden toward what the people dread and commanded toward the Lord, in consecutive verses (Isa 8:12-13).

  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 36:16 (fear)
- **Observation 12567** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): The direction of the trembling: before a threat (A), toward God (Q), for another (P), at another's fall (FE-66).

- **Observation 12648** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Joined with joy, or with silence: "Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice with trembling"; "your heart shall thrill and exult"; "with fear and great joy"; and "they said nothing to anyone, for they were afraid"

- **Observation 12649** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): One root, many renderings: rāgaz (to tremble) rendered "Be angry", "deeply moved", "quarrel"; paḥad (to dread) rendered "thrill"; phobos (fear) rendered "terror", "respect", "reverence", "awe"; ekthambeō (to be alarmed) rendered "greatly amazed", "troubled", "alarmed"; eulabeomai (to be reverent) rendered "afraid", "reverent fear"

- **Observation 12775** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Est 8:17 holds two peoples under one decree: gladness and joy among the Jews, and fear among the peoples, which moved many of them to declare themselves Jews, "for fear of the Jews had fallen on them".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Est 8:17 (fear)
- **Observation 12776** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The main operation in Est 8:17 is fear moving people to change sides: a public declaration made "for fear". The verse gives fear as the only reason; it names no conviction and no God, and the book of Esther never names God.

- **Observation 12777** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Est 8:17 is a third outcome of dread laid on a people, beside Rahab, whose heart melted at what she heard, and Moab, whose dread sent for a curse: taking on the name of the feared, "for fear".

- **Observation 12778** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Est 8:17 and 9:2 are dread with no named giver, so a reading must not say God laid this fear.

- **Observation 12780** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): On the day the enemies "hoped to gain the mastery", "the reverse occurred" (Est 9:1); "No one could stand against them, for the fear of them had fallen on all peoples" (9:2); the officials helped, "for the fear of Mordecai had fallen on them" (9:3).

- **Observation 12781** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The main operation in Est 9:5 is not fear but acting at will ("as they pleased", the M18 word) against those who hated them; fear is in 9:2-3, and 9:2 states its effect: no one could stand.

- **Observation 12782** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): With no one able to stand (Est 9:2), they "did as they pleased" (9:5): 9:5 is the outcome that completes the account of fear.

I will not destroy them".

"Elihu had waited to speak to Job because they were older than he" (32:4), and "I was timid and afraid to declare my opinion" (32:6). Fear and deference hold the anger in, until the others fall silent; Elihu describes the pressure in bodily terms: "I am full of words; the spirit within me constrains me. Behold, my belly is like wine that has no vent; like new wineskins ready to burst. I must speak, that I may find relief" (32:18–20). The held anger is felt as pressure in the belly, and speech is its release.

(Gen 40:6–7).

- **Observation 14323** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): How it co-exists: With the attacked one's inner state named next: "My heart is in anguish within me; the terrors of death have fallen upon me. - **Observation 14975** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Sennacherib as a passage. Mockery of God sent through servants, letters and shouting; its stated aim is to make the hearers afraid (2Ch 32:18). Hezekiah's response: sackcloth, the house of the Lord, the letter spread before God, prayer (2Ki 19:1, 14–19). God's word to the people: "Do not be afraid" (19:6). God names himself as the one mocked (19:22), knows the mocker's raging (19:27), turns the scorn back (19:21), and the mocker goes home with shame of face (2Ch 32:21). Hezekiah calls the day one "of disgrace" (2Ki 19:3, a contempt word, M06).

- **Observation 16793** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 3.1 Service located in a component: [Claude reading] In the NT, slavery is described by which component does the serving. A social slave can serve "from the heart", and so is free where it matters. A free man can be a slave in his flesh (Rom 7:25). The "spirit of slavery" is a spirit of fear (Rom 8:15), which links this batch to Batch A. *kata sarka* "according to the flesh" (Eph 6:5) marks the social relation as the flesh-level one. Phile 16 holds both levels at once.

- **Observation 17872** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): What the verses do with the want. It is not answered with more days. It is answered with the mouth and the will: *"Keep your tongue from evil and your lips from speaking deceit. Turn away from evil and do good; seek peace and pursue it"* (34:13–14). The psalmist is teaching "the fear of the Lord" (34:11). So the want for life is put inside a lesson about fearing God, and it is worked out first in speech.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 34:11 (fear)
- **Observation 17904** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): What lies behind it: fear and abandonment. The psalm opens *"whom shall I fear?"* with an army encamped (27:1–3). Later: *"For my father and my mother have forsaken me, but the Lord will take me in"* (27:10). So the one desire is spoken by someone under threat and left by his nearest.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 27:1 (fear), Psa 27:3 (fear)
- **Observation 17924** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Jeremiah 42 in order.: They ask: *"whatever the Lord our God says … we will do it"* (42:20).; God answers with a promise: *"Do not fear the king of Babylon … for I am with you"* (42:11).; They want the opposite: *"No, we will go to the land of Egypt, where we shall not see war or hear the sound of the trumpet or be hungry for bread"* (42:14).; The reply: *"the sword that you fear shall overtake you there … and the famine of which you are afraid shall follow close after you"* (42:16), and *"in the place where you desire to go to live"* (42:22).
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Jer 42:11 (fear), Jer 42:16 (fear), Jer 42:16 (afraid)
- **Observation 17925** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Compare the sea. *"it would have been better for us to serve the Egyptians than to die in the wilderness"* (Exo 14:12), said as *"they feared greatly"* (14:10). Fear makes slavery preferable. In the wilderness, craving made Egypt look better too: *"For it was better for us in Egypt"* (Num 11:18, in 10.6).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Exo 14:10 (feared)
- **Observation 17926** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Why the relationship matters: the answer is the same.: At the sea: *"Fear not, stand firm, and see the salvation of the Lord"* (Exo 14:13).; In Jeremiah: *"Do not fear … for I am with you"* (Jer 42:11).; The answer to wanting shaped by fear is addressed to the fear, not to the want.; Three passages, at the sea, in the wilderness and after Jerusalem fell, name Egypt as the place wanted back. That is in the verses. I read it as the known past preferred to an unknown future with God. The verses do not say this.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Exo 14:13 (fear), Jer 42:11 (fear)

- **Observation 18836** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Reading: Pilate speaks of his authority when he is afraid (19:8) and has had no answer (19:9). He states it twice, release and crucify. Jesus does not deny it. He names its source, "given you from above", and that changes how guilt is weighed: "therefore" the one who handed him over has the greater sin. After this Pilate seeks to release him, and the crowd moves him with another authority, Caesar's (19:12).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Joh 19:8 (afraid)

- **Observation 18907** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Implicit context. Some of these verses stand inside passages that name the seat of someone else, not the perplexed person. In Joh 13:21, "Jesus was troubled in his spirit" in the verse before the disciples' uncertainty: the seat is Jesus's, and the perplexity is theirs. In 2Cor 4:6, "God … has shone in our hearts", two verses before "perplexed, but not driven to despair". The inner frame there is set by the passage (see also 2Cor 4:16).

- **Observation 18908** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): How the sense shifts. In Mar 6:20, Herod "feared John, knowing … greatly perplexed, and yet he heard him gladly". Four inner operations sit on one bearer with no seat word, each named by a word from a different cluster (fear M01, knowing M15, gladness M04). The operations name themselves. In Gal 4:20 the perplexity is love's: it comes straight after "my little children, for whom I am again in the anguish of childbirth" (4:19).

In the context (14:30), "when he saw the wind, he was afraid, and beginning to sink". The body enacts the doubt. Peter sinks as the trust fails. Seeing leads to fear, which leads to sinking. After the rescue comes worship (14:33).

- **Observation 18917** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 1.4 Anxiety, once: *meteōrizō* G3349 (Luk 12:29): "Nor be worried" (literally "held in suspense, up in the air"). The seat was set at the head of the discourse: Luk 12:22 (seated) reads "do not be anxious about your life (*psychē*)". Every later anxiety in the discourse inherits it. This is the clearest case of a discourse-level seat: stated once, then assumed. The same happens in Mat 6:25 → 6:27–34 (§2).

- **Observation 19189** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where it comes from. In every case a perception of God comes first: "I heard the sound of you in the garden" (Gen 3:10); God names himself: "I am the God of your father" (Exo 3:6); "the terror of the Lord, and … the splendor of his majesty" (Isa 2:10); "the face of him who is seated on the throne, and … the wrath of the Lamb" (Rev 6:16); Inside the person, what meets that perception differs: Adam: knowledge of exposure. "The eyes of both were opened, and they knew that they were naked" (Gen 3:7), and so "I was afraid, because I was naked" (3:10).; Moses: "he was afraid to look at God" (Exo 3:6), with no transgression stated.; The kings, the great, "slave and free": "who can stand?" (Rev 6:17 ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 3:10 (afraid), Exo 3:6 (afraid), Isa 2:10 (terror)
- **Observation 19190** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): How it co-exists. It is always with fear (Gen 3:10; Exo 3:6), terror (Isa 2:10), or wrath felt as unbearable (Rev 6:16–17). In Adam, self-knowledge ("naked") comes between perception and fear. Job 13:20–22 turns the phenomenon inside out. Job names the conditions under which he will *not* hide: "withdraw your hand far from me, and let not dread of you terrify me. Then call, and I will answer". Job shows that hiding is what dread produces. Remove the dread and the person can face God and speak.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 13:21 (dread), Isa 2:10 (terror)

- **Observation 11796** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Made a horror to the kingdoms of the earth.
  - meaning: Meaning: the root names the people or land as the thing that horrifies. The feeling is in the one who passes by and hisses. It stands in lists with "a curse", "a taunt", "a hissing", "a reproach". Implies: this horror is public. A people is exposed before others, and the verses give the cause as their own deeds. Jer 34:17 turns the word "liberty" back on those who withheld it. The inner side is small: it is the onlooker's horror, named in another word.
  - applies to 21 occurrence(s): Deu 28:25 (horror), Deu 28:37 (horror), 2Ch 29:8 (horror), Jer 15:4 (horror), Jer 18:16 (horror), Jer 19:8 (horror), Jer 24:9 (horror), Jer 25:9 (horror), Jer 29:18 (horror), Jer 29:18 (terror), Jer 34:17 (horror), Jer 42:18 (horror); and 9 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 11799** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Horror given to drink, and dismay at the table.
  - meaning: Meaning: the horror is taken inside, as drink: "filled". Dismay goes with the most ordinary eating and drinking. Implies: Eze 12:19 gives the reason, the violence of those who dwell in the land. Ch 13 already carries Eze 12:18–19 (FE-60). These add the dismay word and the cup.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Eze 4:16 (dismay), Eze 12:19 (dismay), Eze 23:33 (horror)
- **Observation 11800** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Dismay for a people's wound.
  - meaning: Meaning: the same root that names a people as a horror to others (FE-86) is here the speaker's own dismay for them. His heart is wounded by their wound. Implies: horror changes character with who holds it: the passer-by hisses, this speaker mourns. R: 8:19 breaks in with God's own voice ("Why have they provoked me to anger …?"). I read 8:21 as the prophet speaking, but the text does not mark the speaker.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 8:21 (dismay)
- **Observation 11802** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Terrors pursuing the wicked, as Job's friends tell it.
  - meaning: Setting: Bildad (18:1) and Zophar (20:1) are two of the friends of whom God says, "you have not spoken of me what is right, as my servant Job has". Job 27:13–23 is Job's own speech. Meaning: the friends set out terrors chasing the wicked as a rule, and apply it to Job. Implies: these verses cannot be read as Scripture's own statement without naming who speaks. I would carry them only with the speaker named, and Job 42:7 beside them. "The king of terrors" follows "the firstborn of death consumes his limbs" (18:13). The death side is held.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Job 18:11 (terrors), Job 18:14 (terrors), Job 20:25 (terrors), Job 27:20 (terrors)
- **Observation 11803** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Terrors at the end, seen from the sanctuary.
  - meaning: Already recorded as life-death LD-154 (with 73:17, "until I went into the sanctuary of God; then I discerned their end"), held for Ch 13.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 73:19 (terrors)
- **Observation 11804** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): A dreadful end.
  - meaning: Setting: Ezekiel's laments over Tyre: "Your heart was proud because of your beauty" (28:17). Job 24:13–17 is Job describing "those who rebel against the light": the murderer, the adulterer, the thief. Meaning: the end is named as dread. Those who chose the dark are "friends with the terrors" of it.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 24:17 (terrors), Isa 17:14 (terror)
- **Observation 11805** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): One event: joy to one, terror to another.
  - meaning: Meaning: the same thing (justice done, the Lord's way) is met as joy or as terror. Implies: the verse names the difference in the one who meets it, not in the event.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 10:24 (dreads), Pro 21:15 (terror)
- **Observation 11806** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status open): "Terror on every side": one phrase in many mouths.
  - meaning: Meaning: the prophet's word is spoken as a name over a man ("a terror to yourself"). The same words are then whispered by his close friends against him. Implies: in both Jer 20 and Psa 31, the one who hears the whisper answers by turning to the Lord ("But the Lord is with me"; "But I trust in you"). The verses state both answers. In Lamentations it is God who summoned the terrors.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Psa 31:13 (terror), Jer 6:25 (terror), Jer 20:3 (terror on every side), Jer 20:4 (terror), Jer 20:10 (terror), Jer 46:5 (terror), Jer 49:29 (terror), Lam 2:22 (terrors)
- **Observation 11807** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Dread that stops a man speaking to God, and a man of clay who answers.
  - meaning: Meaning: Job says dread is what stops him speaking with God. Take it away, and he would speak. Elihu offers himself as one made of clay like Job, whose presence need not terrify. Implies (R): I read Elihu as answering Job's "no arbiter" with his own sameness. The text does not say whether God accepts this. 10.5 and 10.11 already carry 9:34 and 13:20–21. Elihu's answer is new.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 9:35 (fear), Job 13:21 (dread), Job 33:7 (fear)
- **Observation 11808** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "The terrors of God", "your terrors".
  - meaning: Meaning: the terrors are named as God's, and are borne in the spirit (Job 6:4). Psalm 88 sets the terrors beside the waves, and ends with "my companions have become darkness" (Psa 88:18). Implies: Ch 13 Without knowing why carries Job 6:4a and Psa 88:16. The second half of Job 6:4 ("the terrors of God are arrayed against me") and Psa 88:15 widen it. "Close to death" is a death-side link and is held.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 6:4 (terrors), Job 30:15 (terrors), Psa 88:15 (terrors)
- **Observation 11809** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): God asked not to be a terror.
  - meaning: Meaning: the prophet names God as either terror or refuge to him, and asks for refuge. Implies: the same God is named both ways in one breath.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 17:17 (terror)
- **Observation 11810** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Dread at the covenant, and a dream that made a king afraid.
  - meaning: Setting: Gen 15: God then speaks to Abram of his offspring (15:13). Dan 4 is Nebuchadnezzar's own account. Meaning: dread falls in sleep and in visions. The king's fear comes from the visions "of my head".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 4:5 (afraid)
- **Observation 11811** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Moses afraid of God's anger for the people.
  - meaning: Already in Ch 13 (Moses stands between the anger and the people).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 9:19 (afraid)
- **Observation 11812** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status open): Dread laid on peoples and creatures, and what it led to.
  - meaning: Meaning: dread is laid by God, or falls because of his people, and the verses say what it did: the cities did not pursue. Implies: in Rahab, the dread came by hearing, melted heart and spirit, and ends in a confession of God. In Moab, the same dread ends in hiring a curse. Psa 9:20 asks for fear so that the nations know what they are.
  - applies to 13 occurrence(s): Gen 9:2 (fear), Gen 9:2 (dread), Gen 35:5 (terror), Exo 15:16 (terror), Exo 15:16 (dread), Exo 23:27 (terror), Num 22:3 (dread), Jos 2:9 (fear), Psa 9:20 (fear), Isa 19:17 (terror), Isa 19:17 (fear), Dan 5:19 (trembled); and 1 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 11813** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Being feared, and what it does to the feared.
  - meaning: Meaning: Jer 49:16 names the dread Edom inspires, together with the pride of its heart, as what deceived it. Implies: the inner effect of being feared falls on the one who is feared. In Ezekiel 32, the terror once spread is named over the graves. The pit and "the land of the living" are death-side and held.
  - applies to 10 occurrence(s): Pro 20:2 (terror), Eze 26:17 (terror), Eze 32:23 (terror), Eze 32:24 (terror), Eze 32:25 (terror), Eze 32:26 (terror), Eze 32:27 (terror), Eze 32:30 (terror), Eze 32:32 (terror), Hab 1:7 (dreaded)
- **Observation 11814** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status open): What inspires dread in creatures, in visions, and in love.
  - meaning: Setting: Job 39–41 is God's own speech about the creatures he made. Meaning: the dread word describes the thing feared. Implies: in the Song, the same word describes the beloved's beauty.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 41:14 (terror), Job 41:25 (afraid), Job 41:33 (fear)
- **Observation 11815** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): A friend's fear at seeing calamity.
  - meaning: Meaning: Job says his friends have failed him like a dry stream, and names their fear at what they see in him. Implies: two fears stand in one speech: the fear of the Almighty, forsaken when kindness is withheld, and fear at a friend's calamity. R: that the second leads to the failing is Job's reading.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 6:14 (fear), Job 6:21 (afraid)
- **Observation 11817** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Spoken against, each with its ground.
  - meaning: Meaning: each verse gives a ground: the judgment belongs to God; the word failed; God has told it beforehand; there is a higher watcher; "In righteousness you shall be established" (Isa 54:14).
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Deu 1:17 (intimidated), Deu 18:22 (afraid), Isa 44:8 (fear), Isa 44:8 (afraid), Isa 54:14 (fear), Isa 54:14 (terror)
- **Observation 11818** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Afraid of men, and how it ends.
  - meaning: Meaning: the same fear of men has different ends. Ebed-melech is not given into their hands, and the ground is trust. The king is given into them. Implies: Ezr 3:3 says the altar was set up with fear upon them. The ESV "for" makes the fear part of the reason.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Ezr 3:3 (fear), Psa 34:4 (fears), Psa 119:39 (dread), Jer 22:25 (afraid), Jer 39:17 (afraid)
- **Observation 11819** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Job's fear of his suffering, and his comfort in pain.
  - meaning: Meaning: the fear rests on what Job expects of God's verdict. The comfort rests on what he has not denied.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 9:28 (afraid)
- **Observation 11820** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Pangs as of a woman in labor, after a report or a sight.
  - meaning: Meaning: the verses run in order: a report heard or a sight seen; the hands fall, or flight; anguish likened to labor. Implies: Micah asks what is missing within ("Is there no king in you?"), and the labor ends in rescue. The other verses do not go on to rescue.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Exo 15:14 (tremble), Psa 48:6 (trembling), Jer 49:24 (panic)
- **Observation 11822** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status open): Terror instead of peace, and fears brought upon.
  - meaning: Meaning: Isa 66:3–4 sets God's choosing beside theirs: they chose, he chooses. The reason is stated: "when I called, no one answered". Implies: the mind itself is struck (Deu 28:28), as in the curse that gives "a trembling heart" (Deu 28:65; FE-59).
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Deu 28:60 (afraid), Deu 32:25 (terror), Isa 31:9 (terror), Isa 66:4 (fears), Jer 8:15 (terror), Jer 14:19 (terror), Zec 12:4 (panic)
- **Observation 11825** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Idols named by dread words, and trembling for an idol.
  - meaning: Meaning: the terror word (Jer 50:38) and the horror word (1Ki 15:13) name idols. In Hosea, a people tremble for their idol and mourn it.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Hos 10:5 (tremble)
- **Observation 11826** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Terrors in age.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear in old age, beside desire failing. Unit 2's "the keepers of the house tremble" (Ecc 12:3) is from the same passage. Implies: the verse is written toward death ("his eternal home"), so it is held whole.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Ecc 12:5 (afraid), Ecc 12:5 (terrors)
- **Observation 11827** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): God speaks of fearing.
  - meaning: Setting: the Song of Moses. Meaning: God gives a reason for holding back: what the enemy would say. This is the only occurrence in the unit. Implies: under §7a, God in his own terms. It concerns how his act would be read. It says nothing of the human inner being.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 32:27 (feared)
- **Observation 11828** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Awe called for.
  - meaning: Meaning: called for by a psalmist, by a foreign king's decree with its reason ("for he is the living God"), and by Job ("that you may know there is a judgment").
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Job 19:29 (afraid), Psa 22:23 (fear), Psa 33:8 (fear), Dan 6:26 (to tremble), Dan 6:26 (fear)
- **Observation 11830** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Terror mused on afterwards.
  - meaning: Already in 10.1 (a remembered fear turned over).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 33:18 (terror)
- **Observation 11831** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Terror and respect: one root in one passage.
  - meaning: Setting: Rom 13:1–7, on the governing authorities, "instituted by God" (13:1), with "not only to avoid God's wrath but also for the sake of conscience" (13:5). Meaning: the same word is the "terror" a ruler is to bad conduct and the "respect" owed to whom it is due. Implies: the verses tie which it is to the person's own conduct: "do what is good" or "if you do wrong".
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Rom 13:3 (terror), Rom 13:3 (fear), Rom 13:4 (afraid)
- **Observation 11832** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Do not be afraid", and the ground given each time.
  - meaning: Meaning: as in the Hebrew (unit 1, face C), the "fear not" comes with a reason. Here the reasons are: a prayer heard, favour found, good news, "it is I", a calling, the Father's good pleasure. Implies: 10.1 What changes it says the reason is mostly God himself. The Greek verses add the speaker's own presence ("it is I") and a task given ("you must stand before Caesar"; "you will be catching men").
  - applies to 15 occurrence(s): Mat 1:20 (fear), Mat 14:27 (afraid), Mat 28:5 (afraid), Mat 28:10 (afraid), Mar 6:50 (be afraid), Mar 16:6 (alarmed), Luk 1:13 (afraid), Luk 1:30 (afraid), Luk 2:10 (fear), Luk 5:10 (afraid), Luk 12:32 (fear), Joh 6:20 (afraid); and 3 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 11833** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The word of "fear not" with a touch.
  - meaning: Meaning: in these three verses the fear is met by a hand as well as a word. Implies: this is the hand acting toward the one who fears. It bears on OT-06 (God's hand, the inner being acting through the hands). "As though dead" (Rev 1:17) is a likeness, not a death-side statement.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 17:7 (fear), Rev 1:17 (fear)
- **Observation 11834** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear set against faith, as a question.
  - meaning: Meaning: Jesus puts fear and faith against each other, by question, in three tellings of the storm and in Peter on the water. Hebrews 11 states the reverse: by faith, not afraid. Implies: Heb 11:27 gives what the not-fearing rested on: "as seeing him who is invisible". In Mat 14:30 the order is stated: he saw the wind, he was afraid, he began to sink, he cried out. Mat 14:31 names doubt, which links to M20 (10.11, Ch 11).
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Mat 8:26 (afraid), Mat 14:30 (afraid), Mar 4:40 (afraid), Mar 5:36 (fear), Luk 8:25 (afraid), Luk 8:50 (fear), Heb 11:23 (afraid), Heb 11:27 (afraid)
- **Observation 11835** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Afraid of the storm, then afraid of the one who stilled it.
  - meaning: Meaning: the fear before the storm is rebuked as lack of faith. The fear after the calm is greater ("great fear"), and is a question about who he is. Implies: this is the Greek instance of the factor already in 10.1 What changes it: what the fear is turned toward (Exo 14:10, 31; Jon 1:5, 16). It is the same people and, in Mark, the same root (phobeomai, to fear, with phobos, fear), with a different object, after the act.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mar 4:40 (afraid), Mar 4:41 (fear), Luk 8:25 (afraid)
- **Observation 11836** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear at an act, and glorifying God.
  - meaning: Meaning: in each verse the fear (or "awe") and the giving of glory, or a confession, are stated together. Implies: the verses say this, and it is not my join. The earthquake and opened tombs around Mat 27:54 (27:51–53) are death-side and held.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Mat 9:8 (afraid), Luk 7:16 (fear), Act 19:17 (fear), Rev 11:13 (terrified), Rev 15:4 (fear)
- **Observation 11837** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear at the same kind of act, and asking him to go.
  - meaning: Meaning: here the fear before Jesus' act, or before a word about judgment, ends in asking him to go. Peter's "Depart from me" gives his own reason ("I am a sinful man"), and he is answered with "Do not be afraid". Implies: set beside FE-127, the verses state two ends of fear before the holy: glorifying God, or "depart". The verse gives no reason for the Gerasenes beyond "great fear".
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Mar 5:15 (afraid), Luk 5:10 (afraid), Luk 8:35 (afraid), Luk 8:37 (fear), Act 24:25 (alarmed)
- **Observation 11838** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear at the tomb: with joy, and with silence.
  - meaning: Meaning: two accounts of the women's fear. In Matthew it is joined with joy, and they run to tell. In Mark, fear is the reason given for saying nothing. Implies: I set them side by side. I do not harmonise them; each states its own. Mar 16:7 had just told them to "go, tell his disciples and Peter".
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Mat 28:8 (fear), Mar 16:5 (alarmed), Mar 16:6 (alarmed), Mar 16:8 (trembling), Mar 16:8 (afraid), Luk 24:5 (frightened)
- **Observation 11839** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear of people that silences, hides and separates.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear of people stops speech (Joh 7:13), keeps a disciple secret (Joh 19:38), locks a door (Joh 20:19), and draws a man back from the table (Gal 2:12). Joh 9:22 states what was feared: being put out of the synagogue. Implies: Gal 2:13 names what followed as hypocrisy. At the locked door, the answer is a presence and a word ("Peace be with you"), and then "the disciples were glad when they saw the Lord" (Joh 20:20).
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Joh 7:13 (fear), Joh 9:22 (feared), Joh 19:38 (fear), Joh 20:19 (fear), Gal 2:12 (fearing)
- **Observation 11840** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear of the crowd holding back a desired act.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear of the people holds back the hand: the arrest is not made, force is not used. The intent is still stated: "seeking to arrest", "wanted to put him to death". Implies: fear changes the act, and the verses do not say it changes the wish. Mat 21:25–26 shows the fear inside a reasoning ("If we say …").
  - applies to 9 occurrence(s): Mat 14:5 (feared), Mat 21:26 (afraid), Mat 21:46 (feared), Mar 11:18 (feared), Mar 11:32 (afraid), Mar 12:12 (feared), Luk 20:19 (feared), Luk 22:2 (feared), Act 5:26 (afraid)
- **Observation 11841** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Herod and John, in two accounts.
  - meaning: Meaning: Mark names Herod's fear of John himself, grounded in what he knew of him. Matthew names Herod's fear of the people. Implies: I set them side by side, unreconciled. Mar 6:20 is already in 10.1 and 10.2.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 14:5 (feared), Mar 6:20 (feared)
- **Observation 11842** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Afraid of a person, and someone who stands between.
  - meaning: Meaning: the fear is grounded in not believing what Saul now is. It is met by another person's testimony.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 9:26 (afraid)
- **Observation 11843** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Afraid to ask.
  - meaning: Meaning: each time Jesus foretells his death, the verses set not understanding beside a fear that keeps them from asking. Implies: Luk 9:45 is already in 10.2. Mar 10:32 does not name what they feared (R). The death side of the sayings is held.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mar 9:32 (afraid), Mar 10:32 (afraid), Luk 9:45 (afraid)
- **Observation 11844** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Fear and trembling": one phrase in seven settings.
  - meaning: Meaning: in these seven verses the phrase goes with coming forward, obedience, a sincere heart, telling the whole truth, and asking to be saved. In none of the seven does it drive the person away. Implies: the Hebrew trembling at Sinai made the people stand far off (10.1). This phrase, in these verses, does the reverse. To the woman Jesus says, "Daughter, your faith has made you well; go in peace" (Mar 5:34).
  - applies to 12 occurrence(s): Mar 5:33 (fear), Mar 5:33 (trembling), Luk 8:47 (trembling), Act 16:29 (fear), 1Cor 2:3 (fear), 1Cor 2:3 (trembling), 2Cor 7:15 (fear), 2Cor 7:15 (trembling), Eph 6:5 (fear), Eph 6:5 (trembling), Phili 2:12 (fear), Phili 2:12 (trembling)
- **Observation 11845** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear and love, in their setting.
  - meaning: Setting: 1Jo 4:16–18, "God is love". Meaning: read with 4:17, the fear that love casts out is fear in view of "the day of judgment", which "has to do with punishment". Implies: 10.1 quotes 4:18 alone, as the verse that comes closest to saying what fear itself is. The setting narrows it: the verse speaks of fear before judgment, set against the confidence love gives. I would restate the 10.1 sentence so that it does not claim more than the verse.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 1Jo 4:18 (fear), 1Jo 4:18 (whoever fears)
- **Observation 11846** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The spirit not of fear; the Spirit of adoption; the slavery of fear.
  - meaning: Meaning: in Romans fear goes with slavery, set against crying "Abba! Father!". In 2 Timothy, read with 1:6 ("fan into flame the gift of God, which is in you") and 1:8, the spirit not of fear is what lets him not be ashamed and share in suffering. Implies (claim 9-4): the register already restates 9-4: "spirit of …" names a disposition, and 2Ti 1:7 says "God gave us a spirit not of fear". The Greek reading supports that restatement and adds the setting (shame and suffering for the gospel). It does not name a spirit-being of fear. Heb 2:15 is in 10.1. Its death side is held.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Rom 8:15 (fear), 2Ti 1:7 (fear), Heb 2:15 (fear)
- **Observation 11847** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Two fears, and "Fear not", in one speech.
  - meaning: Meaning: in a few verses, Jesus forbids fear of men, commands fear of God, and then says "Fear not", grounded in the Father's care for the smallest things. Implies: this is the NT counterpart of Exo 20:20 and Isa 8:12–13 in 10.1 What changes it: one fear taken away, another set in its place, by the same speaker. The soul-and-body side ("cannot kill the soul") is in Ch 2, 3 and 9 and is held under OT-03 / OT-08.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Mat 10:26 (fear), Mat 10:28 (fear), Mat 10:31 (fear), Luk 12:4 (fear), Luk 12:5 (fear), Luk 12:7 (fear)
- **Observation 11848** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fearing God as a standing, in Acts and the letters.
  - meaning: Meaning: as in the Hebrew (unit 1, face D), fearing God is a standing, stated with giving, praying, walking, doing right, holiness and sincerity of heart. Implies: two joinings are new to the Greek: fear with "the comfort of the Holy Spirit" (Act 9:31), and fear as a reason to persuade others (2Cor 5:11). "God-fearing" is also used as a description by others (Act 10:22; 13:16, 26).
  - applies to 12 occurrence(s): Luk 1:50 (fear), Act 9:31 (fear), Act 10:2 (feared), Act 10:22 (fearing), Act 10:35 (fears), Act 13:16 (fear), Act 13:26 (fear), 2Cor 5:11 (fear), 2Cor 7:1 (fear), Col 3:22 (fearing), Rev 11:18 (fear), Rev 19:5 (fear)
- **Observation 11849** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Father" and fear, side by side.
  - meaning: Meaning: 1 Peter joins calling God "Father" with conduct "with fear", and gives the reason: he "judges impartially". Romans sets "fear" against crying "Abba! Father!". Implies: I set them side by side only. No verse joins them. Each names a different fear, by its own words: the fear of slavery (Rom 8:15); conduct before an impartial judge (1Pe 1:17).
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Rom 8:15 (fear), 1Pe 1:17 (fear)
- **Observation 11850** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear called for, as a guard.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear is called for in place of pride (Rom 11:20), as a guard against falling short (Heb 4:1), as the effect of a public rebuke (1Ti 5:20), and because judgment has come (Rev 14:7). Implies: Rom 11:20 sets "fear" directly against "proud", and grounds it in "the kindness and the severity of God" (11:22).
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Rom 11:20 (fear), 1Ti 5:20 (fear), Heb 4:1 (fear), 1Pe 2:17 (fear), Rev 14:7 (fear)
- **Observation 11851** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Reverence: Noah, worship, and a consuming fire.
  - meaning: Meaning: reverence (eulabeia, reverence) is the manner in which Noah acted on a warning about what he could not see, and the manner of worship. Heb 12:29 gives its ground. Implies: eulabeomai (to be cautious, reverent) is also the tribune "afraid that Paul would be torn to pieces" (Act 23:10). One root: reverent fear that builds, and fear for another's life.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 23:10 (afraid)
- **Observation 11852** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Jesus' own distress, and his reverence.
  - meaning: Meaning: the root rendered "troubled" for Jesus in Gethsemane (ekthambeō, to be alarmed) is rendered "greatly amazed" for the crowd that ran to greet him (Mar 9:15) and "alarmed" for the women at the tomb (Mar 16:5). "Do not be alarmed" is said to them (16:6). Hebrews names his reverence as the reason he was heard. Implies: this is the root's change of character across three settings. "Even to death" and "save him from death" are death-side and held. Heb 5:7 is already in Ch 2 and Ch 7.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mar 14:33 (troubled), Mar 16:5 (alarmed), Mar 16:6 (alarmed)
- **Observation 11853** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Sinai, the bush, and what the readers have come to.
  - meaning: Meaning: Heb 12 sets the terror of Sinai against what the readers have come to, and then still calls for "reverence and awe, for our God is a consuming fire" (12:28–29). Heb 10:27 and 10:31 are said of one who goes "on sinning deliberately after receiving the knowledge of the truth" (10:26). Implies: the setting of Heb 10:31, which 10.1 quotes, is deliberate sin after knowledge. 10.1 does not yet say so.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Act 7:32 (trembled), Heb 10:27 (fearful), Heb 10:31 (fearful thing), Heb 12:21 (tremble), Heb 12:21 (fear)
- **Observation 11854** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear at a messenger from God.
  - meaning: Meaning: each appearance brings fear, and in most the messenger speaks to the fear first. Implies: at the tomb in Matthew, the guards tremble, and the word "Do not be afraid" is said "to the women". Cornelius answers his fear with a question ("What is it, Lord?"). "Like dead men" is a likeness.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Mat 28:4 (fear), Mat 28:5 (afraid), Luk 1:12 (fear), Luk 1:13 (afraid), Luk 2:9 (fear), Luk 2:10 (fear), Act 10:4 (terror)
- **Observation 11855** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear when what is seen is taken for something else.
  - meaning: Meaning: the verses state the wrong reading in the fear: "It is a ghost!"; "thought they saw a spirit". The answer is "It is I", and being shown and touched. At the transfiguration, fear goes with not knowing what to say (Mar 9:6), and Peter's word is "not knowing what he said" (Luk 9:33). Implies: fear and a mistaken seeing go together here. The Gospel says so, by stating what they thought.
  - applies to 10 occurrence(s): Mat 14:26 (fear), Mat 14:27 (afraid), Mat 17:6 (terrified), Mat 17:7 (fear), Mar 9:6 (terrified), Luk 9:34 (afraid), Luk 24:37 (startled), Luk 24:37 (frightened), Joh 6:19 (frightened), Joh 6:20 (afraid)
- **Observation 11856** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear coming on a whole community, by hearing.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear spreads by hearing: "all who heard of it". Luk 1:65–66 joins the fear with talk, with laying the things up "in their hearts", and with a question. Implies: Act 5:3–4 names what Ananias did as in the heart: "why has Satan filled your heart", "contrived this deed in your heart". Ananias' death is death-side and held. Rev 11:11 ("a breath of life from God entered them … great fear fell on those who saw them") is held whole.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Luk 1:65 (fear), Act 5:5 (fear), Act 5:11 (fear), Rev 11:11 (fear)
- **Observation 11858** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Have no fear of them" and "with gentleness and respect": one word, two verses apart.
  - meaning: Meaning: in 1Pe 3:14–15 the same root is forbidden (fear of those who make you suffer) and asked for (the "respect" of the answer). The verse puts honouring Christ "in your hearts" between them. Implies: this is the clearest case in the unit of the word changing character within one sentence. (Counted against this unit only; Rom 13:3–7 is the other.)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 1Pe 3:6 (fear), 1Pe 3:14 (fear)
- **Observation 11859** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Not fearing God.
  - meaning: Meaning: Rom 3:18 closes a chain of quotations ("None is righteous", 3:10). The judge names his own not-fearing to himself, and acts from another motive. The criminal asks the other about fearing God, gives his reason, and turns to Jesus. Implies: the criminal's question joins fearing God to owning his own guilt ("we indeed justly"). The setting is a death; that side is held.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Luk 18:2 (feared), Luk 18:4 (fear), Luk 23:40 (fear), Rom 3:18 (fear), 2Pe 2:10 (tremble)
- **Observation 11860** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Standing far off in fear of another's torment.
  - meaning: Meaning: the fear is at Babylon's judgment, and it keeps them at a distance, weeping. Implies (R): "stood far off" also describes the people at Sinai (Exo 20:18, in 10.1). I note the shared wording only.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Rev 18:10 (fear), Rev 18:15 (fear)
- **Observation 11861** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The end of the age: fainting with fear, and "raise your heads".
  - meaning: Meaning: one set of events. Some faint with fear and foreboding. The disciples are told not to be terrified and to raise their heads, with a reason: "your redemption is drawing near". Implies: a second instance of FE-95's factor (who meets it).
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Luk 21:9 (terrified), Luk 21:11 (terrors), Luk 21:26 (fear)
- **Observation 11862** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): An apostle's fear for a church.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear for others (face P), with its object stated: their thoughts led astray, their quarrels, labour in vain. Implies: 2Cor 7:5–6 names "fear within" and its comfort, which came through a person's arrival. 2Cor 11:3 is in 10.4. 2Cor 7:5 is in Ch 7.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Cor 7:5 (fear), 2Cor 11:3 (afraid), 2Cor 12:20 (fear), Gal 4:11 (afraid)
- **Observation 11863** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Not fearing in suffering, said of oneself and to others.
  - meaning: Meaning: the ground is God's word that he will not leave, or "I am with you". Heb 13:6 is self-spoken, as Psa 56:4 is in 10.1 Meeting fear. Act 18:9 ties the fear to silence: "go on speaking". Implies: "unto death" (Rev 2:10) is held.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Act 18:9 (afraid), Phili 1:28 (frightened), Heb 13:6 (fear), Rev 2:10 (fear)
- **Observation 11864** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear in a man's considering, and fear met in a dream.
  - meaning: Meaning: Joseph's fear is inside his deliberation ("as he considered these things"; 1:19, "resolved to divorce her quietly"). Both times a dream answers it.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 1:20 (fear), Mat 2:22 (afraid)
- **Observation 11865** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Demons believe, and shudder.
  - meaning: Setting: James on "faith apart from works" (2:18, 20). Meaning: the demons are named as believing that God is one, and shuddering. James uses it to show that such belief alone is not the faith he means. Implies: under the style guide (#1924), other spirits are dealt with in their own terms and not watered down: the verse states belief and shuddering in demons, and no more.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 2:19 (shudder)
- **Observation 11866** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Deilos: afraid in the boat, cowardly in the list.
  - meaning: Meaning: one root family appears as a question to the disciples in the storm, as what Jesus' peace is set against, as what God's gift is not, and at the head of the list in Rev 21:8 (already in 10.1). Implies: I note that in Rev 21:8 "the cowardly" stands next to "the faithless", and in Mar 4:40 the same root stands next to "Have you still no faith?". I note it only; no verse explains one by the other.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Mat 8:26 (afraid), Mar 4:40 (afraid), Joh 14:27 (afraid), 2Ti 1:7 (fear)
- **Observation 11867** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The servant afraid of a hard master, in Luke.
  - meaning: Meaning: Matthew's version (Mat 25:24–25) is already in 10.11, 11 and 13. Luke adds the master's answer: the servant is judged "with your own words". Implies: the fear rested on what the servant said he knew of his master.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 25:25 (afraid), Luk 19:21 (afraid)
- **Observation 11868** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Officials afraid on learning who a man is; sailors afraid of the sea.
  - meaning: Meaning: the officials' fear rises on hearing who the man is, and what they have done to him. Pilate's rises on hearing "he has made himself the Son of God" (Joh 19:7). In the storm, the sailors' fear leads to anchors and prayer for day. Paul's word rests on what he was told.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Joh 19:8 (afraid), Act 16:38 (afraid), Act 22:29 (afraid), Act 27:17 (fearing), Act 27:24 (afraid), Act 27:29 (fearing)
- **Observation 11869** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Mercy with fear.
  - meaning: Meaning: fear is the manner in which mercy is shown. R: the verse does not say what or whom the fear is toward.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jude 23 (fear)
- **Observation 11870** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear named among the fruits of godly grief.
  - meaning: Already in 10.1 and 14. 2Cor 7:10 sets it: "godly grief produces a repentance that leads to salvation".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 7:11 (fear)
- **Observation 11872** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear is felt in the heart and the body together.
  - meaning: Fear is felt in the heart and the body together. "he was afraid, and his heart trembled greatly"; "My flesh trembles for fear of you, and I am afraid of your judgments"; "Fear and trembling come upon me"; Saul "fell at once full length on the ground, filled with fear"
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): 1Sa 28:5 (afraid), 1Sa 28:5 (trembled), 1Sa 28:20 (fear), Psa 55:5 (fear), Psa 55:5 (trembling), Psa 55:5 (horror), Psa 119:120 (fear), Psa 119:120 (afraid)
- **Observation 11873** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear leads to hiding.
  - meaning: Fear leads to hiding. "I was afraid, because I was naked, and I hid myself"; "Moses hid his face, for he was afraid to look at God"
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Gen 3:10 (afraid), Exo 3:6 (afraid)
- **Observation 11874** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear leads to flight
  - meaning: Fear leads to flight
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Exo 2:14 (afraid), 1Sa 17:24 (afraid), 2Ki 25:26 (afraid), Jer 26:21 (afraid)
- **Observation 11875** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear holds back from speaking: "Samuel was afraid to tell the vision to Eli"; the servants "were afraid to tell him that the child was dead"; Elihu "was timid and afraid to declare my opinion"
  - meaning: Fear holds back from speaking: "Samuel was afraid to tell the vision to Eli"; the servants "were afraid to tell him that the child was dead"; Elihu "was timid and afraid to declare my opinion"
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 1Sa 3:15 (afraid), 2Sa 12:18 (afraid), Job 32:6 (afraid)
- **Observation 11876** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status resolved): Fear holds back from an act: the young man "did not draw his sword, for he was afraid"; the armor-bearer "would not, for he feared greatly"; "the people feared the oath"
  - meaning: Fear holds back from an act: the young man "did not draw his sword, for he was afraid"; the armor-bearer "would not, for he feared greatly"; "the people feared the oath"
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Judg 8:20 (afraid), 1Sa 14:26 (feared), 1Sa 31:4 (feared), 1Ch 10:4 (feared)
- **Observation 11877** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear leads to wrong.
  - meaning: Fear leads to wrong. "I feared the people and obeyed their voice"; "he feared to say, 'My wife'"; "Whom did you dread and fear, so that you lied, and did not remember me"; Sarah "denied it … for she was afraid"
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Gen 18:15 (afraid), Gen 26:7 (feared), Gen 31:31 (afraid), 1Sa 15:24 (feared), Isa 57:11 (fear)
- **Observation 11878** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear is used against a person as a tool.
  - meaning: Fear is used against a person as a tool. "he was hired, that I should be afraid and act in this way and sin"; "they all wanted to frighten us, thinking, 'Their hands will drop from the work'"; spoken "to frighten and terrify them, in order that they might take the city"
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): 2Sa 14:15 (afraid), 2Ch 32:18 (frighten), Neh 6:9 (frighten), Neh 6:13 (afraid), Neh 6:14 (afraid), Neh 6:19 (afraid)
- **Observation 11879** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear can grow into enmity.
  - meaning: Fear can grow into enmity. "Saul was afraid of David because the Lord was with him" → "Saul was even more afraid of David. So Saul was David's enemy continually"
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:12 (afraid), 1Sa 18:29 (afraid)
- **Observation 11880** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear can be turned to God in the moment.
  - meaning: Fear can be turned to God in the moment. "When I am afraid, I put my trust in you" (56:3) is followed by "in God I trust; I shall not be afraid" (56:4). "Jehoshaphat was afraid and set his face to seek the Lord"
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Ch 20:3 (afraid), Psa 56:3 (afraid), Psa 56:4 (afraid), Psa 56:11 (afraid)
- **Observation 11881** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The feared thing can overtake the one who flees it: "the sword that you fear shall overtake you … and the famine of which you are afraid shall follow close after you"
  - meaning: The feared thing can overtake the one who flees it: "the sword that you fear shall overtake you … and the famine of which you are afraid shall follow close after you"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 42:16 (fear)
- **Observation 11882** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear spreads to others: the "fearful and fainthearted" man is sent home "lest he make the heart of his fellows melt"; "Whoever is fearful and trembling, let him return home"
  - meaning: Fear spreads to others: the "fearful and fainthearted" man is sent home "lest he make the heart of his fellows melt"; "Whoever is fearful and trembling, let him return home"
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Deu 20:8 (fearful), Judg 7:3 (fearful), Judg 7:3 (trembling)
- **Observation 11883** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The same root, in the same episode, changes object.
  - meaning: The same root, in the same episode, changes object. The mariners "were afraid" in the storm, and "were exceedingly afraid" when they learned whom Jonah fled. After the sea is calmed, "the men feared the Lord exceedingly, and they offered a sacrifice to the Lord and made vows". At the sea, "they feared greatly" as Egypt marched; after the crossing, "the people feared the Lord, and they believed"
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Exo 14:10 (feared), Exo 14:31 (feared), Jon 1:5 (afraid), Jon 1:10 (afraid), Jon 1:16 (feared)
- **Observation 11884** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Afraid before God, and naming the place.
  - meaning: Afraid before God, and naming the place. Jacob "was afraid and said, 'How awesome is this place! This is none other than the house of God'". David "was afraid of the Lord that day" after Uzzah (already in 10.1). The people "were afraid because of the fire" and did not go up the mountain
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Gen 28:17 (afraid), Exo 34:30 (afraid), Deu 5:5 (afraid), 2Sa 6:9 (afraid), 1Ch 13:12 (afraid)
- **Observation 11885** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Do not fear" in the mouth of one who means harm.
  - meaning: "Do not fear" in the mouth of one who means harm. Jael: "turn aside to me; do not be afraid", before the tent peg. Absalom: "Strike Amnon … Do not fear; have I not commanded you? Be courageous". ~~Gedaliah's "Do not be afraid to serve the Chaldeans" stands before his murder (Jer 41:2)~~ Corrected while weaving (2026-10-01): Gedaliah spoke in good faith and was himself the one murdered, so he does not belong here. Jer 40:9 and 2Ki 25:24 stay under face C, as "do not fear" with a reason
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Judg 4:18 (afraid), 2Sa 13:28 (fear)
- **Observation 11886** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Fear not" is also spoken to the land and the beasts: "Fear not, O land; be glad and rejoice"; "Fear not, you beasts of the field"
  - meaning: "Fear not" is also spoken to the land and the beasts: "Fear not, O land; be glad and rejoice"; "Fear not, you beasts of the field"
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Joe 2:21 (fear), Joe 2:22 (fear)
- **Observation 11887** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The one who does not fear has a firm heart: "He is not afraid of bad news; his heart is firm, trusting in the Lord. His heart is steady; he will not be afraid" (already in 10.1).
  - meaning: The one who does not fear has a firm heart: "He is not afraid of bad news; his heart is firm, trusting in the Lord. His heart is steady; he will not be afraid" (already in 10.1). "though an army encamp against me, my heart shall not fear". "I will fear no evil, for you are with me". "The Lord is on my side; I will not fear. What can man do to me?"
  - applies to 11 occurrence(s): Psa 3:6 (afraid), Psa 23:4 (fear), Psa 27:1 (fear), Psa 27:1 (afraid), Psa 27:3 (fear), Psa 46:2 (fear), Psa 91:5 (fear), Psa 91:5 (terror), Psa 112:7 (afraid), Psa 112:8 (afraid), Psa 118:6 (fear)
- **Observation 11888** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear of man is questioned: "who are you that you are afraid of man who dies, of the son of man who is made like grass".
  - meaning: Fear of man is questioned: "who are you that you are afraid of man who dies, of the son of man who is made like grass". "do not fear what they fear" (Isa 8:12). The next verse, "Let him be your fear, and let him be your dread" (Isa 8:13), uses other Strong's numbers and comes in a later unit

  - meaning: It is learned, by hearing and reading: "hear and learn to fear the Lord your God"; the king reads the law "that he may learn to fear the Lord his God"

  - meaning: Its absence is laid at God's hardening in one prayer: "why do you make us wander from your ways and harden our heart, so that we fear you not?"

- **Observation 11900** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): It governs conduct toward the weak and toward others.
  - meaning: It governs conduct toward the weak and toward others. In Leviticus it stands as the "but": "You shall not curse the deaf or put a stumbling block before the blind, but you shall fear your God"; "You shall not wrong one another, but you shall fear your God"; "You shall not rule over him ruthlessly but shall fear your God". He stood in awe of my name"
  - meaning: It is joined with joy, delight and trembling: "Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice with trembling"; "who delight to fear your name"; "Blessed is the man who fears the Lord, who greatly delights in his commandments"; "a covenant of fear, and he feared me. He stood in awe of my name"

- **Observation 11903** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Feared and not feared, of the same people.
  - meaning: Feared and not feared, of the same people. "So they feared the Lord but also served their own gods" (17:33), and the next verse: "They do not fear the Lord" (17:34). Earlier in the chapter: they "did not fear the Lord" and were taught "how they should fear the Lord"
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): 2Ki 17:25 (fear), 2Ki 17:28 (fear), 2Ki 17:32 (feared), 2Ki 17:33 (feared), 2Ki 17:34 (fear), 2Ki 17:41 (feared)
- **Observation 11904** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status open): Those who fear the Lord gather and speak: "Then those who feared the Lord spoke with one another. The Lord paid attention"; "I am a companion of all who fear you"; "Those who fear you shall see me and rejoice"
  - meaning: Those who fear the Lord gather and speak: "Then those who feared the Lord spoke with one another. The Lord paid attention"; "I am a companion of all who fear you"; "Those who fear you shall see me and rejoice"
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Psa 22:25 (fear), Psa 66:16 (fear), Psa 119:63 (fear), Psa 119:74 (fear), Psa 119:79 (fear), Mal 3:16 (feared)
- **Observation 11905** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): It is joined with God's wrath: "Who considers the power of your anger, and your wrath according to the fear of you?"
  - meaning: It is joined with God's wrath: "Who considers the power of your anger, and your wrath according to the fear of you?"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 90:11 (fear)
- **Observation 11907** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear laid on nations: "I will begin to put the dread and fear of you on the peoples"; "they shall be afraid of you" because "you are called by the name of the Lord"; "I will put fear in the land of Egypt"; "cities of ruthless nations will fear you"; "let all the ends of the earth fear him"
  - meaning: Fear laid on nations: "I will begin to put the dread and fear of you on the peoples"; "they shall be afraid of you" because "you are called by the name of the Lord"; "I will put fear in the land of Egypt"; "cities of ruthless nations will fear you"; "let all the ends of the earth fear him"
  - applies to 14 occurrence(s): Deu 2:4 (afraid), Deu 2:25 (dread), Deu 2:25 (fear), Deu 2:25 (tremble), Deu 28:10 (afraid), Psa 33:8 (fear), Psa 67:7 (fear), Psa 72:5 (fear), Psa 102:15 (fear), Isa 25:3 (fear), Isa 59:19 (fear), Jer 10:7 (fear); and 2 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 11908** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear of other gods is forbidden by name: "You shall not fear other gods … but you shall fear the Lord your God"
  - meaning: Fear of other gods is forbidden by name: "You shall not fear other gods … but you shall fear the Lord your God"
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Judg 6:10 (fear), 2Ki 17:7 (feared), 2Ki 17:35 (fear), 2Ki 17:36 (fear), 2Ki 17:37 (fear), 2Ki 17:38 (fear), 2Ki 17:39 (fear)
- **Observation 11909** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Not fearing, as freedom: Job, "Then I would speak without fear of him"; "you will be secure and will not fear"; "shall not fear destruction when it comes"; "She is not afraid of snow for her household"
  - meaning: Not fearing, as freedom: Job, "Then I would speak without fear of him"; "you will be secure and will not fear"; "shall not fear destruction when it comes"; "She is not afraid of snow for her household"
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Job 5:21 (fear), Job 5:22 (fear), Job 9:35 (fear), Job 11:15 (fear), Pro 3:25 (afraid), Pro 3:25 (terror), Pro 31:21 (afraid)
- **Observation 11910** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fear of the Lord with a prayer for his work: "your work, O Lord, do I fear. In the midst of the years revive it"
  - meaning: Fear of the Lord with a prayer for his work: "your work, O Lord, do I fear. In the midst of the years revive it"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Hab 3:2 (fear)
- **Observation 11927** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Fretting = heating oneself
  - meaning: Fretting = heating oneself at the wicked's success. It "tends only to evil". It is replaced by trust, committing, stillness and waiting
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 37:1 (fret), Psa 37:7 (fret), Psa 37:8 (fret), Pro 24:19 (fret)
- **Observation 11954** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Answered by turning to God instead
  - meaning: Answered by turning to God instead: be still and wait; ponder on your bed and be silent; leave it to the wrath of God and feed the enemy
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 37:7 (fret), Psa 37:8 (fret)
- **Observation 12049** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): Anger's course and company.
  - meaning: Jacob curses the anger, not the men, and keeps his soul out of their council. Anger is learned by company. Cherished anger goes with no cry for help. Bildad says Job tears himself. Jealousy is set beyond anger. Psa 37:7 before 37:8: "Be still before the Lord and wait patiently for him".
  - chain: company → learn his ways → snare (Pro 22:24–25); fret → evil (Psa 37:8)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 37:7 (fret), Psa 37:8 (fret)
- **Observation 12094** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): What ḥēmāh adds in the sayings: a person named by it.
  - meaning: "a man of wrath" and "a hot-tempered man" (literally a man of heat) name a person by the word. It is something to "forsake" (Psa 37:8), and it "turns away" with time (Gen 27:44). Pro 19:19 says delivering such a man must be repeated.
  - implication: unit 1 (AG-51 to AG-53, AN-14, AN-27, AN-28) covers these sayings. ḥēmāh adds that the word can become what the person is called.
  - chain: hot-tempered → strife (Pro 15:18) · man of wrath → strife; given to anger → transgression (29:22) · man of great wrath → penalty; deliver him → again (19:19) · wrath → evil (Psa 37:8)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 37:8 (fret)
- **Observation 12136** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): "Fret not yourself": the burning verb turned on oneself.
  - meaning: the reflexive form of ḥārāh: to heat oneself (10.1 already says so). All four are about evildoers or the one who prospers; two set it beside envy (Psa 37:1; Pro 24:19).
  - implication: the reason given is what the evildoer's end is (Pro 24:20) and what the fretting does: "it tends only to evil" (Psa 37:8). Psa 37 sets trust, commit, be still in its place (37:3, 5, 7).
  - chain: fret → tends only to evil (Psa 37:8) · fret, envy → "the evil man has no future" (Pro 24:19–20)
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 37:1 (fret), Psa 37:7 (fret), Psa 37:8 (fret), Pro 24:19 (fret)
- **Observation 12191** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, status draft): The same words, with no anger: troubled faces.
  - meaning: zāʿap̄, which is rage in 2Ch 26:19, is here a downcast face (Gen 40:6) and a thinner face (Dan 1:10). sāʿar, the storm, is a king's troubled mind and a city's affliction. None of these verses is about anger.
  - implication: a change of character within one root (#1919). Correction: Ch 11 §4 Storm calls 2Ki 6:11 the angry king's mind. The verse says "greatly troubled"; no anger word is in it.
  - chain: they were troubled → "Why are your faces downcast?" → "We have had dreams" (Gen 40:6–8) · mind greatly troubled → "who of us is for the king of Israel?" (2Ki 6:11)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 40:6 (troubled)

## 5. Claims and the narrative

66 claims rest on these occurrences: 1 on an observation of these words and 65 reached only because one of their sources is a verse where a member word stands. The second group is about its verse, not about the collection; it is kept so that nothing stored is hidden, and labelled.

### 5.1 Claims resting on an observation of these words (1)

- **C-349** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Fear, a melted heart, grief and jealousy hold the will in a refusal, and what a person then hears can move it.
  - beyond the text: gathers four feelings under one effect on the will, and reads David's change as moved by what he heard
  - rests on: observation 11876 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 10:4, 1Sa 14:26, 1Sa 31:4, Judg 8:20)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Willing

## 6. Phrases attached to the collection (12)

A phrase is attached by the route in its link reason (a Strong's code or an English word shared with a member) and is a member of the collection: its occurrences are in sections 2 and 3 under their Strong's codes, and what the study recorded for them in sections 4 and 5. Each is shown here with its route, its occurrences and the live observation nodes whose word is the phrase.

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 0 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
